like the sun. When she saw him, she reached out her hand to him and said to her: "Raise me up and give me water to drink." He raised her (up), gave her water to drink, led her to his dwelling, and brought a physician for her; (the latter) healed her and she was in much better health than before. The Egyptian loved her and said to her: "Come, sleep (with me)." 'Arwä said to him: "Fear God and love your soul; do you not think of the day of Judgment? I will not sleep with you, for I have a husband and I will not do injury to my husband." The Egyptian left her and went out. A Hebrew, servant of the Egyptian, came and said to her: "I want to marry you." 'Arwä said to him: "Go away from me, dog!" The Hebrew left her. The Egyptian had a small child, an only son, and, apart from this one, he had none (other). The Hebrew servant of the Egyptian killed this small child with a knife; he brought the small child where 'Arwä was lying, laid the small child near her and, as for the knife, he placed it in her hand; indeed, the Hebrew wanted them to kill 'Arwä because she had refused to sleep (with him). When the mother of the small child woke up, she did not find her son; while she wanted to nurse the small child, she looked for him and found him dead near the bed of 'Arwä whom the Hebrew servant had implicated; as for her, she was sleeping. The mother of the small child went out weeping (and shouting) at the top of her voice. All the people gathered and entered to 'Arwä; the Egyptian said to her: "Why have you done me harm and killed my son? I brought you back to life from the dead and I raised you from the tomb!" 'Arwä said to the Egyptian: "You did me good and you raised me from the dead. Why would I have killed your son in exchange for the good that you did me? Would I have rewarded you badly? Previously, you asked me to marry you, I feared the Lord and refused you (it). Would I commit a greater sin: killing a soul? I did not kill your son." (The Egyptian) said to her: "Go away, depart from me." She departed from him, he gave her (beforehand) for her viaticum four hundred pieces (of silver) and said to her: "Go away, I cannot control myself in this matter, I know what happened to me."

She went away and, traveling, arrived at a city. She saw a man hanging from a tree and said to those who were hanging him: "Why have you hung this man?" They said to her: "Because he deprived us of our goods." She said to them: "To how much (do) the goods amount of which he deprived you?" They said to her: "(To) four hundred pieces (of silver)." She gave (them) to them and said to them: "Take him down from his cross." (The man) went toward them and they said to him: "Who repaid for you your debt of four hundred pieces (of silver)? If we, (we were) your father, we would not have repaid. It is a widow woman who repaid for you." The man, an adolescent, went away, following 'Arwä; he found her and said to her: "What (do you think of it)? I want to sleep with you." 'Arwä said to him: "I saved you from the fire which (is) on earth, you, you want to make me enter into the fire which (is) in the heavens and you do not consider any good for me!" When he saw that she feared God, he left her and went to several men (who were going to leave) on a boat at sea; he called them and

ወይቤሉ ፡ ይንግሥ ፡ ለነ ። ወእምድኅረ ፡ ፈጸሙ ፡ ነገሮሙ ፡ አእረፈ ፡ ንጉሥ ። ወእምድኅሬሁ ፡ አንገሥዋ ፡ ለአርዋ ፡ እንዘ ፡
said to them: "I have a servant whom I bought with much of my money, I will sell her to you if you give me money." When they saw the radiance of the beauty of 'Arwä and the (beautiful) aspect of her face—one (of these) men had much gold, forty thousand pieces—the man whom she had redeemed from the crucifixion sold her to this (man) rich in gold and the one who had bought her took her, made her board the boat, loved her, wanted to sleep with her, and took her by the hand. The sun darkened, the winds blew and swirled, the wave rose and was near to submerging the boat. All those who (were) in the boat cried, wept, and begged God. 'Arwä said: "Praise to God, he who will commit himself to him will not die and he who will have confidence in him will not perish." 'Arwä said to those who (were) in the boat: "Of him who sold me unjustly nor (of) him who bought me unjustly am I the servant, on the contrary I am free; now, if you do what I tell you, I will ask God that he guide you out of this peril." They said to her: "We will do what you tell us." She said to them: "He who bought me, let him not sleep with me; God does not desire that thing." (To) them all, they disbursed ten thousand pieces (of silver), gave (them) to the one who had bought her, and made her free. She begged God, the wave became silent, the wind stopped, and everything became calm. Again, all desired her and wanted to sleep with her, saying: "Me, me!" Immediately, a violent wind occurred and broke their boat: they were dispersed and fell into the sea. 'Arwä remained alone and all their goods remained with her. She made pants, dressed like a man, and pulled her hair. She traveled by boat and arrived at a large island, the people of the country (lived) in the middle of the sea. She went and arrived at the king; when the king saw 'Arwä, she seemed to him (to be) a young adolescent whose beard had not (yet) grown. She said to the king: "Here are many goods, a boat full. Those who possessed these goods perished and fell into the sea, I remained, I adolescent; behold, you see me, the only one to have survived. All their goods that survived (are) with me, in my hand, I fear that they might perish and I want you to take (them); I, I will write a letter and carry (it) to the country of their relatives to each one: their parents will come here and will take their goods, each one his own." When the king saw that she had not acted unjustly with regard to their goods and that he saw the radiance of her beauty, he was astonished and she seemed to him (to be) an adolescent; the king took all her (= 'Arwä's) goods. The fear of God entered into 'Arwä and God heard her supplication. Many sick were healed by her prayer. She went to the edge of the sea, entered a cave, and remained (there) begging God. The king of the island fell ill and arrived at the edge of death. All the people gathered, came to their king, and said to him: "Behold, you are going to die, to whom will you hand over to reign over us after you?" (The king) answered and said to them: "There is a young adolescent who brought me many goods in a boat, that one, make him reign for me over you after me." All agreed (to this) and said:

"Let him reign over us." After they had finished their speech, the king died. They made 'Arwä reign after him who seemed to them (to be) a young adolescent. She prepared herself to reign and the love of royalty entered into her heart. She reflected again in her heart, saying: "This royalty will distance me from God." And she wanted to refuse them (to be king). She gathered the people and said to them: "You who have a daughter and a sister, adorn them with gold, with precious clothes, and with jewels and bring them to me. The one that I will have chosen among them, let her be my wife." All brought, as she had ordered them, their sisters and daughters, having adorned (them), and placed (them) for 'Arwä before her. The parents went out, leaving their daughters with her. 'Arwä took off her clothes and stood before the young girls. When (these) saw the radiance of her beauty (and) that she was a woman, they were astonished, were amazed, and said: "We have never seen among all the people a woman who (was) like her!" 'Arwä said to the young girls: "Go, speak to your parents and say to them: Will a woman have power over you M (like?) formerly (a woman) sat on your royal throne." The people said: "It pleases us that she who does not marry a man reigns, and she who does not take the money of men, let her reign over us." She

refused them (to be king) and returned to (her) cave. In those days, her history spread in all the countries, namely that she begged God and healed the sick by her prayer. After long days, the husband of 'Arwä returned to his house and found his brother ill, blind, and paralyzed. He said to his brother: "What have you done with my wife 'Arwä?" (His brother) said to him: "We found your wife sleeping with a man and we stoned her and killed her." The husband of 'Arwä was silent. After many days, her history spread, namely that she healed the sick by her prayer. The brother of the husband of 'Arwä went to him, he begged him and said to him: "There is a woman in an island, she heals the sick by her prayer and God listens to her prayer. Lead me there." His brother said to him: "Will you be able to arrive there?" The husband of 'Arwä said to him: "Let us go (to see) if God will have mercy on you." They both went. Found themselves together the young man whom ('Arwä) had redeemed from his crucifixion with four (hundred) pieces (of silver)—he had become blind—and the Hebrew servant who had killed the son of his master and who had placed the knife in the hand of 'Arwä; this servant, his Egyptian master was leading him—he too was ill and blind. As for the young man whom ('Arwä) had redeemed from his crucifixion, his father was leading him. As for the brother of her husband, who had stoned her, his brother, the husband of 'Arwä, was leading him. All arrived before her and entered to her; 'Arwä recognized them all, but they did not recognize 'Arwä. 'Arwä returned to her cave, (then) returned to them.


[p. 356] {#po.47.p356}
ወይቤላ ፡ ምታ ፡ ለአርዋ ፡ ናሁ ፡ እሁበኪ ፡፣ ንዋየ ፥ ኩሎ ፡ ዘፈቀድኪ ፡ ፈውሲ ። ወትቤሎ ፡ ንዋየ ፡ በዲበ ፡ ድውያን ፡ ኢነሣእኩ ፡ ወበኀቤከሰ ፡ እነሥእ ፡ ወባሕቱ ፡ ብዙኅ ፡ ኃጢአቱ ፡ ለእቱከ ፡ ይንግር ፡ ኩሎ ፡ ዘገብረ ። ወይቤ” ፡ ምታ ፡ ለአርዋ ፡ ሠናየ ።
The husband of 'Arwä said to her: "Behold, I will give you all the money that you want, heal (my brother)." She said to him: "I have not taken money from the sick, but from you, I will take (some); only great (is) the sin of your brother.

Let him tell everything that he has done." The husband of 'Arwä said: "This woman has spoken well, only tell, (my brother,) all your sins." The brother of the husband of 'Arwä said to him: "Your wife remained refusing me what I asked her, namely to sleep with me. She refused me (it) and I produced against her four lying witnesses; I stoned her and killed her." The husband of 'Arwä said to him: "You acted badly toward her, you who killed my wife. [We have the same father and same mother as for what is past, what would I do (about it)? May (God) forgive you." 'Arwä begged God: his (= her brother-in-law's) eyes opened, his feet were untied, and all (the body) of the one who had killed 'Arwä was healed. The father of the one she had redeemed from the crucifixion also said to 'Arwä: "My son has become blind. Beg God (for him)." 'Arwä said to him: "Great (is) the sin of your son. Let your son confess the sin that he has committed and I, I will beg (God for him)." The blind man said to her: "You have spoken well, I confess my fault: a woman who was passing on the road came, she found me hanging and redeemed me for four hundred pieces (of silver); I loved her and wanted to sleep with her, she refused me (it). I saw people at sea in a boat and sold them 'Arwä for ten thousand pieces (of silver). When I returned from them, my eyes went blind and the pieces (of silver) became stones." 'Arwä said to him: "While previously you were a liar, God has made you speak sincerely." She begged God: the man was healed and his eyes opened. The Egyptian master of the Hebrew also came and said to him (= to 'Arwä): "You see, this servant of mine has become blind; beg God so that he may heal." The Egyptian did not know that 'Arwä was the one he had raised from the tomb. 'Arwä said to him: "Great (is) his (= the Hebrew's) sin; because of that, he has become blind and ill. Now, let him confess everything that he has done and I, I will beg (God) for him." The master of the Hebrew said to him: "Tell your fault; if you do not tell (it), I will leave you here and return to my country." The Hebrew said to his master: "An Israelite woman remained at our house, I asked her to sleep with her and she refused me (it). I killed your son, brought him to her while she was sleeping, and laid him with her; as for the knife, I placed it in her hand, wanting you to kill her because she had refused to sleep (with me)." When (the Hebrew) had confessed his sin, ('Arwä) begged God and (the man) was healed on the spot.
ወሐለዉ ፡ ሀየ ፡ ሰብአ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ገዚረት ፡ ወባዕድኒ ፡ እለ ፡ ኀቤሃ ፡ ወፈጺማ ፡ ነገሮሙ ፡ አተዉ ፡ ብሔሮሙ ። ወምታ ፡ ባሕቲቱ ፡ እንዘ ፡ ሀለወ ፡ ምስሌሃ ፡ ቀርበት ፡ ኀቤሁ ፡ ወትቤሎ ፡ ብእሲትከ ፡ እንተ ፡ ትብል ፡ ኪያሃ ፡ ኪያየኑ ፡ ትመስል ። ወተሰጥወ ፡ ምታ ፡ ወይቤላ ፡ አንፍኪ ፡ ወቀራንብትኪ ፡ ወአይንኪ ፡ ይመስላ ፡ እንቲአሃ ። ወሶበ ፡ ኢሞተት ፡፤ ወኢተቀብረት ፤ እመሰልኪ ፡ ኪያሃ ፡ ወእምአኀዝኩኪ ፡ በአዴየ ፡ ወእምወሰድኩኪ ። ወትቤ ፡ አነ ፡ ውእቱ ፡ ብእሲትከ ፡ አርዋ ፡ ዘአጥፍኡኒ ፡ በሐሰት ፡ ወተቀተልኩ ፡ ወተሠየጥኩ ፡ በእንቲአከ ፡ ፀሐየ ፡ ወሞ'ረ ፡ ወሐፈ ፡ ተዓገሥኩ ፡ በእንቲአከ ፡ ባሕረ ፡ ወየብሰ ፡ ሖርኩ ፡ በእንቲአከ ። ወሶበ ፡ ነገረቶ ፡ ዘንተ ፡ ከመዝ ፡
There were people from the island there, as well as others who (had come) to her; when ('Arwä) had finished their business, they returned to their country. Her husband being alone with her, she approached him and said to him: "Does your wife, the one you call (by that name), resemble me?" Her husband answered and said to her: "Your nose, your eyelids, and your eyes resemble hers. If she were not dead and buried, you would appear (to be) her; I would take you by my hand and lead you away." She said: "I am your wife 'Arwä, whom they destroyed with a lie; I was killed and I was sold because of you; I endured the sun, the cold, and the heat because of you; I traveled the sea and the land because of


[p. 360] {#po.47.p360}
ተደሙ ፡ ወበከዩ ፡ ክልኤሆሙ ። ወእምድኅሬሁ ፡ ሰአላ ፡ ከመ ፡ ይስክብ ፡ ምስሌሃ ። ወአበየቶ ፡ ወትቤሎ ፡ ኢይከውነኒ ፡ ይእዜ ፡ እትሐጸብ ፡ ወእምጻእ ፡ ኀቤከ ። ወተንሥአት ፡ ወቦአት ፡ ውስተ ፡ በዓታ ፡ ወተሐፅበት” ፡ ወለብሰት ፡ አልባሰ ፡ ሐዲሰ ። ወሰአለት ፡ ኀበ ፡ እግዚአብሔር ፡ ወትቤ ፡ እመሰ ፡ ታፈቅረኒ ፡ ተመጠዋ ፡ ለነፍስየ ፡ ዘእንበለ ፡ እስክብ ፡ ዳግመ ፡ ምስለ ፡ ብእሲ ፡ ወሰገደት ፡ ወሞተት ። ወቦአ ፡ ምታ ፣ ወረከባ ፡ ምውተ ፡ ወቀበራ ፡ ምታ ። በጸሎታ ፡ ለአርዋ ፡ ይምሃሮ ፡ ለንጉሥነ ፡ ዮሐንስ ፡ ወለኩልነ ፡ ሰማዕያን ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን ። ወበዛቲ ፡ ዕለት ፡ ካዕበ ፡ ተዝካሩ ፡ ለዲማዲስ ፡ ሰማዕት ። ይምሐሮ ፡ በጸሎቱ ፡ ለፍቁሩ ፤ አትናቴዎስ ፡ ወለፍቅርቱሂ ፡ ኮቶሊካዊት ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን ።
you." When she had told him this, they were astonished and both wept. After that, he asked her to sleep with him. She refused (him) and said to him: "(That) is not possible for me now; I am going to wash myself and come to you." She rose, entered her cave, washed herself, and put on new clothes. She prayed to God and said: "If you love me, receive my soul before I sleep with a man again." And she prostrated herself and died. Her husband entered and found her dead; her husband buried her. May (God), through the prayer of 'Arwä, have mercy on our king Yohannes and on all of us, listeners (to this story), in the ages of ages, amen. [On this day also, (there is) the commemoration of Dimädis the martyr. May (God) have mercy through his prayer on his dear 'Atnätéwos and also on his dear Kotolikäwit in the ages of ages, amen.]
ተፈጸመ ፡ በዝየ” ፡ ምንባበ ፣ ወርኃ ፡ ግንቦት ፡ ቡሩክ ፡ ወስብሐት ፡፤ ለእግዚአብሔር ፡ 'ወላዕሌነ ፡ ይኩን ፡ 'ሣህሉ ፣፤ ወምሕረቱ” ፡ ለዓለመ ፡ ዓለም ፡ አሜን።
Here is completed the reading of the blessed month of Genbot. Glory to God! May his mercy and his pity be upon us in the ages of ages, amen.
